━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

要备份的内容（在 BE 上或远程 GPU 服务器上）：
  ✅ 代码（含 .git 历史）
  ✅ 训练 / 评测脚本
  ✅ 训练得到的 checkpoints（你自己训出来的，不是 HF / Meta 公开权重）

  ✅ 论文相关产物（latex 源、figures、PDF）
  ✅ 论文用到的中间产物（图表生成脚本、统计 CSV）

❌ 不要备份：
  - 公开数据集（HuggingFace datasets、GLUE、SuperGLUE 等 anyone 都能 re-download 的）
  - 公开预训练模型（LLaMA、Qwen、GPT-2、BERT 等）

你这个项目的 GPU 服务器：
  - rtx5090-4x
  - bjb2-12179  (账号: root)

收集策略：
本项目有 2 台 GPU 服务器（rtx5090-4x / bjb2-12179）。

a) 在 BE 上指定一个临时打包目录: ~/faith-evidence-ie_archive/
b) 从每台 GPU 服务器 rsync 数据回来: ~/faith-evidence-ie_archive/&lt;server_name>/
c) 全部到位后再打 tar.gz 上传

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 2 步：打包成 tarball
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

把第 1 步收集到的目录全部打成 .tar.gz：

  TS=$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)
  cd ~ && tar czf /tmp/faith-evidence-ie-final-$TS.tar.gz faith-evidence-ie_archive/

确认 tarball 大小合理（一般几百 MB ~ 几十 GB）：

  ls -lh /tmp/faith-evidence-ie-final-*.tar.gz

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

S3 位置（你这个项目专属）：

  s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/manager-backup/backends/faith-evidence-ie/project-archive/

凭证：BE 上的 `~/.agent-ml-be-backup.yaml` 已经配好，可以直接读。

在 BE 上跑这个上传脚本（aws CLI 没装，用 boto3）：

```python
# 保存为 /tmp/upload_to_s3.py，然后执行：python3 /tmp/upload_to_s3.py /tmp/faith-evidence-ie-final-*.tar.gz
import yaml, boto3, sys, time
from pathlib import Path

cfg = yaml.safe_load(open('/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-be-backup.yaml'))
s3 = boto3.client('s3',
    aws_access_key_id=[REDACTED]'aws_access_key_id'],
    aws_secret_access_key=[REDACTED]'aws_secret_access_key'],
    region_name=cfg['s3_region'])

src = sys.argv[1]
size = Path(src).stat().st_size
key = f'manager-backup/backends/faith-evidence-ie/project-archive/' + Path(src).name
print(f'Uploading {src} ({size/1024/1024:.1f} MB) → s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/{key}')
t0 = time.time()
s3.upload_file(src, 'agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp', key)
elapsed = time.time() - t0
print(f'✅ UPLOAD OK: {elapsed:.1f}s ({size/1024/1024/elapsed:.1f} MB/s)')
print(f'S3 URI: s3://agent-ml-manager-backups-emnlp/{key}')
print(f'File size: {size} bytes')
```

执行：

━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━
第 4 步：汇报
━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━━

  - 你不需要、也无法在 S3 上验证文件是否存在
  - boto3 upload_file 没抛异常 = 上传成功（HTTP 200 + ETag 校验通过）

Since the rtx5090 server has more comprehensive data including the 14B model and eval outputs, I'll prioritize syncing from there first, then check bjb2 for anything exclusive. My approach is to compress and transfer the training outputs and project directory from rtx5090-4x to the backend, verify what's unique on bjb2, and if there's additional data worth keeping, transfer that as well before uploading everything to S3.

The compressed archive should come to around 23GB total—the safetensors files won't compress much, maybe 10-20% reduction, plus the project directory. That's manageable, so I'm going to start the tar process on rtx5090-4x now.

- sft_baseline_eval_results, sft_eval_r3_results, sft_eval_results (eval result dirs)

Unique to rtx5090:
- grpo_ced_g4_bf16 (vs bjb2's grpo_bf16_g4)


- Several rsft and sft output directories with different naming schemes (rsft_output_14b variants, rsft_output_1_7b_s44, rsft_output_ced versions, sft_output_14b variants)
- eval_outputs/ directory

Most of the rsft/sft directories appear on both machines, so the main differences are bjb2's eval result directories and the specific output variants on rtx5090.

Now I'm checking whether grpo_bf16_g4 on bjb2 is actually unique compared to rtx5090's grpo_ced_g4_bf16 — they look like different runs. The unique content on bjb2 includes the grpo_bf16_g4 directory with checkpoints (around 3.5GB total, maybe 1.5GB without intermediate checkpoints), plus several sft evaluation result directories and various log files. I'm thinking about whether to tar everything from bjb2 with the same checkpoint exclusions to get a complete backup, though that means some duplication with rtx5090. The real constraint is fitting both backups on the storage device.

Looking at the sizes, rtx5090's tarball would be roughly 18-22GB compressed, and bjb2 has about 22GB of final adapters with significant overlap. If I tar both, I'm looking at potentially 40GB+ of tarballs, which exceeds the available space. A better approach would be to rsync from rtx5090 as the primary source since it likely has the superset of data, then selectively grab only the truly unique items from bjb2 to avoid redundancy. I'm looking at a list of model output variants across different configurations and seeds, then identifying which files are unique to the bjb2 system compared to rtx5090 so I can compress those into a tar archive.

While waiting for the rtx5090 tar to finish, I should also grab the local project workspace with the paper LaTeX, figures, and configs from the faith-evidence-ie directory, since that's important research data to preserve.

Wait, I need to recalculate — the tarball is already included in that 19GB used, so if it grows to 20GB total, I'd need 31GB on a 30GB disk. That won't work. I should explore whether I can pipe the tar directly or split it across multiple files to avoid storing the full thing on the remote server at once. I'm realizing the compressed tarball will likely hit around 20GB when I factor in the freige directory and its nested contents, plus the safetensors files that only compress by 5-10%. That puts me right at the disk limit with the system overhead, so streaming the tar directly through SSH to avoid writing a temporary file on the remote server is definitely the way to go.

I'll kill the current partial tarball and use a piped approach instead — creating the archive on the fly while excluding checkpoints, cache directories, and other unnecessary files, then compressing and transferring it all in one command so nothing gets stored locally on the remote machine. Writing the tar command...

- 1GB code/configs/json → 0.3GB compressed
